The outward shell extends [only so far], but the wise person can behold its hidden, secret center. The Blood comes from our *Green Lion* <note>original: *Green Lyon*. A famous alchemical symbol usually representing the raw, unrefined state of the matter, or sometimes a powerful solvent like "acid of antimony" or "vitriol."</note>, which is indeed the greenest—or rawest—of the three. For it has no kind of metallic *Sulfur* <note>original: *Sulphur*. In alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, fiery, and masculine principle of matter.</note>—not even a single grain—and therefore it is *totally volatile* <note>original: *Totally Volatile*. This refers to a substance that easily evaporates or escapes when heated, rather than remaining solid or "fixed" in the fire.</note>. It is even rawer than common water, and yet it is called "the Blood" for a most secret reason: because it is the seat of life, which is the Spirit, just as blood in a human is the seat of their life. 

Indeed, the Spirit is made manifest by this "Soul" of our *Green Lion* and is united to it. So, although it may be very green or unripe, it is inhabited by that which is both pure and ripe, which can and will digest <note>original: *digest*. In alchemy, "digestion" is the process of exposing a substance to steady, gentle heat over a long period to mature or transform it.</note> it with the Water, and make both become life joined with life. 

Now, the Spirit is nothing other than a *Chaos* <note>original: *Chaos*. A term often used for the "First Matter" (Prima Materia)—the raw, disorganized substance from which the Philosopher's Stone is created.</note>—the wonder of the wonders of God—which almost every person possesses and yet does not know it. This is because, as it appears to the world, it is contained within a lowly, despised form. Yet it is so useful that in human affairs, no one can do without it. To the philosopher, it appears united to the Blood—that is, of our *Green Lion*—which truly is not a Lion until the

<summary>Sir George Ripley explains that the "Green Lion" represents a raw, volatile substance containing a hidden spiritual "Blood" or life force. This "Chaos," though appearing in a common and despised form, is the essential spirit required for alchemical digestion and transformation.</summary>

...then most perfect, which Nature alone could never achieve. Nor is there anything that can elevate Tinctures <note>original: exalt Tinctures. In alchemy, "exalting" means to raise a substance to a higher state of purity or power; "tinctures" are the spiritual or metallic essences used for transformation.</note> in this way except for our dissolving Water, which I told you flows from three springs. 

One is a common well from which everyone draws, and many people use this water. This well contains a "Saturnine" dross <note>original: Saturnine drossiness. Impurities associated with lead (Saturn), representing a heavy, sluggish, or crude state of matter.</note> which makes the water useless. These cold impurities are purged by two other springs through which the water from the first well is artificially made to flow. 

These two springs actually form just one well, whose waters appear dry because their moisture is sealed away. The well itself is surrounded by an "Arsenical" wall <note>original: Arsenical Wall. Likely a metaphor for a toxic, volatile, or protective chemical barrier.</note>; its slimy bottom is full of the First Essence <note>original: First Ens. The fundamental "being" or spiritual core of a substance.</note> of mineral salt and sulfur. These substances sharpen <note>original: acuate. To increase the chemical potency, acidity, or "sharpness" of a liquid.</note> the water from the first well, which is naturally cold. Once sharpened in this way, it becomes such a powerful solvent <note>original: Menstruum. An alchemical term for a solvent used to dissolve solid matter.</note> and is so compatible with metals that, because of its unique power, it is chosen to be the "Bath of the Sun and Moon" <note>original: Bath of the Sun and Moon. A poetic and technical term for the liquid medium used to dissolve gold (the Sun) and silver (the Moon) during the Great Work.</note>.

> ->*For I will now truly encourage you to understand well the three <term>Mercuries</term>.*<-

But because one book is never sufficient in this mastery <note>original: Mastery. Here referring to the Great Work or the art of alchemy.</note> to reveal everything that must be known, and other authors write differently about <term>Mercury</term>, pay further attention to what I have to say to you concerning this point.

We have in our work, properly speaking, three *Mercuries*. One of these is to be prepared by the philosopher (which I have already mentioned). When this is joined with the "perfect body" <note>original: perfect Body. Usually refers to gold or silver, the stable metallic base used in the process.</note> and set to digest <note>original: digest. To heat a substance gently over a long period to induce chemical change.</note>, the glass vessel is sealed. Then, in this first composition, the matter is called *Rebis* <note>original: Rebis. From the Latin "res bina," meaning "double thing" or "two-fold matter." It represents the union of opposites.</note>—that is, "two things" in number—because you can still separate one from the other in their entire nature.

Once these two are joined, they react within the vessel until the compound becomes a black powder, which is then called the "Ashes of the Platter" <note>original: Ashes of the Platter. A symbolic name for the stage of *nigredo*, or blackness, signifying the death or putrefaction of the old form.</note>.

This powder dissolves <note>original: relenteth. To soften, melt, or turn into liquid.</note> into a black broth, which is called *Elixir*, or water extracted by elixation <note>original: Elixation. The process of boiling or simmering a substance in a liquid to extract its medicinal or spiritual essence.</note>, which is repeated liquefaction.

### The Keys of this Science.

These *menstruums* <term>menstruum</term> <note>original: Menstruums. Derived from the Latin word for "monthly," in alchemy this refers to a solvent or liquid used to dissolve and transform solid substances.</note> or *Mercuries* are the very keys of this science: the first is the Philosopher's Key, and the other two are Nature’s Keys.

->*Raymond calls them his Menstruums.*<-
<note>original: Reymund his Menstrues doth them call. "Reymund" refers to Raymond Lully (Ramon Llull), a major figure in medieval alchemy and logic.</note>

They are called *menstruums* by wise men for three reasons: first, for their secrecy. Just as the monthly cycles of women are hidden from common view, so these *Mercuries* are hidden from common seekers. Secondly, for their prophetic signs: just as those cycles in women indicate the maturity to conceive, so these are called *menstruums* because they are fit for

procreation. Thirdly, regarding their function: just as those in women are considered nutritional for the embryo, so our "Child" <note>In alchemy, the "Child" or "Offspring" refers to the developing Stone or the result of the union of substances.</note> is nourished by these to reach full age and strength.

Let me add a fourth reason, which concerns timing: the "Philosophical Preparation" will rarely provide your first *menstruum* <term>menstruum</term> <note>original: Menstruum. In the fourth month, it will overflow with power, and with it, you will soon see the perfected *Sulphur* of Nature, for it is the "Fire of Nature"; and in this first state of "Exaltation" <note>Exaltation: A process by which a substance is raised to a higher state of purity or power.</note>, the White Stone <note>The White Stone (or White Elixir) is the penultimate stage of the Great Work, capable of transmuting base metals into silver.</note> is perfected.

->*Without them, truly no Truth is accomplished.*<-

He, then, who does not know the secret of our *menstruums* <term>menstruums</term> <note>original: Menstruals. He is like a man who would try to enter an inaccessible castle without a key, or shoot a bow without a string.

Now, so that you may know our secrets exactly, we shall faithfully reveal our experience to you, as sincerely as one brother might speak to another; and I shall reveal what I have never yet found disclosed in any author.

In our *Mercury*, there are three Mercurial substances which may well be called *menstruums*. One is the coarser part (which, although it is a water, is the most tangible and visible part, and so may be termed the Body of the Water). The last is a fiery form, which is the

Blood of *Cadmus* <note>original: Cadmus. In Greek mythology, Cadmus slew a dragon; in alchemy, the "Blood of Cadmus" represents a potent, transformative substance or the "soul" of the metal.</note>; this is a real invisible form, which is essentially and formally **Volatile Sun** <note>original: Sol Volatile. But that which is superficial is visible and can be seen, and is more material and passive. Now, those first two which are superficial are the Water and the Blood; the essential *menstruum* is the Spirit. All three are united as one; they are distinct in number, though not in kind.

### *That bird Essential to Sun and Moon.*

So then, two are material, passive substances which are united in our philosophical <note>original: sophical</note> *Mercury*. The third is an active essence which is hidden in our *Mercury*, which is essential to the *Sun* and *Moon* <note>In alchemy, the Sun (Sol) and Moon (Luna) represent gold and silver, or the active male and passive female principles.</note>, because it is a Fire, which is **Volatile Sun** <note>original: Sol volatile. This refers to the spiritualized or "volatile" essence of gold.</note>. As the Artist <note>The alchemist.</note> governs this *Mercury*, it will digest the passive principles into either the *Sun* or the *Moon* <note>original: Sol or Luna</note> at the philosopher's pleasure.

### *Their Properties I will declare right soon.*

I shall soon, in the proper place, describe to you all the properties of these three *menstruums* <term>menstruums</term> <note>original: Menstrues. These are alchemical solvents used to dissolve and transform solid matter.</note> when I come to that section. In the meantime, take notice that by this *Mercury*, in which there are three *Mercuries* or *menstruums*, the perfect bodies <note>Referring to the "perfect" metals, gold and silver.</note> will be calcined <term>calcined</term> <note>Reduced to a powder through the application of heat.</note> and then dissolved into *Mercury*. This substance is not then properly called a *menstruum*, for it is the fruit itself, called *Azoth* <note>A universal solvent or the essential transformative agent in alchemy.</note> or *Virgin's Milk* <note>A symbolic name for a white, liquid stage of the alchemical process.</note>, which is a state of refinement <note>original: digestion</note> beyond the *menstruums*.

*And Mercury of Metals essential,*
*Is the Principle of our Stone material.*

When the [perfected] bodies are dissolved, they transform the previously mentioned *mercuries* into their own nature by their own catalyst <note>original: ferment</note>. This happens because the "Fire of Nature" assimilates everything that nourishes it into its own likeness. Then, our *mercury* or solvent <term>menstruum</term> <note>original: Menstrue</note> disappears—that is, it is swallowed up into the solar nature <note>The nature of gold (Sol).</note>. Together, they form one "universal Mercury" through an intimate union, and this *mercury* is the material principle of the [Philosopher's] Stone. 

Before this, our *mercury* (being composed of three *mercuries*) contained two components that were superficial and a third that was essential only to gold <note>original: Sol</note> and silver <note>original: Luna</note>, but not yet to the Stone itself. Nature would normally produce these two out of it by artificial boiling <term>decoction</term> <note>original: decoction; the process of heating or "cooking" a substance to extract its essence or mature it.</note>. However, once the perfect bodies are dissolved, they transform the *mercury* that dissolved them. At that point, there is no more resistance <note>original: repugnancy; here meaning a conflict or lack of harmony between the substances.</note> within the substance. There is no longer a distinction between what is superficial and what is essential, for everything has become essential. And this is the single "matter" of the Stone.

> *This is the Stone we are referring to,*
> *for whoever understands our writings correctly.*

Truly, anyone who has studied our books well will understand that this universal Mercury, which we call *Azoth* <term>Azoth</term> <note>original: Azoth; a name for the universal solvent or the essential mercury of the alchemists, considered the beginning and end of the work</note>, is indeed our Stone. It only lacks "digestion" <term>digestion</term> <note>the process of maturing a substance through gentle, continuous heat, similar to how food is broken down in the stomach</note>, for it is inseparably united. 

This union is not a "dyptative" conjunction <term>dyptative</term> <note>a two-fold union; likely from the Greek "dyas" for two</note>, which is merely a mixture of gold <note>original: the Sun</note> with our *Mercury*. Nor is it a "triptative" union <term>triptative</term> <note>a three-fold union; from the Greek "trias" for three</note>, which is the mixture and union of the Body, Soul, and Spirit occurring before "putrefaction" <term>putrefaction</term> <note>the stage where the matter turns black and decomposes before being "reborn" in a purified state</note>. Instead, it is a "tetraptive" union <term>tetraptive</term> <note>a four-fold union; from the Greek "tetra" for four</note>, which is the "anatization" <term>anatization</term> <note>original: Anatization; the equalization or total blending of the four primary qualities (hot, cold, moist, and dry) into a single, balanced substance</note> of qualities. This is the first degree of the "white Stone" <note>the stage of the Great Work capable of transmuting base metals into silver</note>, which will then grow higher and higher in power until the "Moon" <note>the silver or white stage of the process</note> reaches its full maturity.

> *It is a bright soul and substance.*

This Stone, or its power to multiply <term>virtue multiplicative</term> <note>the ability of the finished Stone to transform a much larger quantity of base metal into gold or silver</note>, is not related to the physical matter itself, but to its "form" <term>form</term> <note>in the philosophy of the time, the "form" was the essential identity or soul of a substance that gave it its specific properties</note>. This form allows the matter to receive and later impart "tinctures" <term>tinctures</term> <note>the transformative colors or spiritual essences that change the nature of metals</note>. For who could believe that gold <note>original: Sol</note>, in which the virtue...

whereby the power <note>original: virtue</note> is merely singular <note>original: unary</note>—meaning it is only sufficient for itself—unless by the addition of our *Mercury* (which, in regard to its material parts, is below the level of gold <note>original: Sol</note> and requires digestion <term>digestion</term> <note>the process of maturing a substance through gentle, continuous heat</note> only to ripen it to the height of gold), I say that by the mixture of those two Tinctures of Venus <term>Venerial Tinctures</term> <note>original: Venerial Tinctures; referring to the attractive or "loving" qualities of the substances that allow them to bond; in alchemy, Venus is the force of union</note>, that power should be multiplied in an almost infinite manner.

### *Of the Subtle Influence of Gold and Silver.*

If this Tincture—which in the *Mercury* exists as gold <note>original: Sol</note> and silver <note>original: Luna</note>—were not like a Soul (that is, a spiritual thing), this multiplication would be impossible. It is, therefore, the very "gift of fertility" <note>original: *Dos fœcunditatis*</note> which exists within minerals. This fertility appears in their silver and gold essences <note>original: Lunary and Solary Tinctures</note> and was planted in them during the first blessing of "Increase and Multiply" <note>original: *Crescite & Multiplicamini*; a reference to the biblical command in Genesis 1:28</note>. While the growth of some things happens "according to quantity" <note>original: *juxta quantitatem*</note>, this increase is an improvement in quality.

*Through which the Earth receives resplendence.*

So then, the matter of minerals is a dead, passive thing, within which is included a Light that is clothed in a *vital etheric breath* <note>original: *vitali Aura ætheria*</note>, so to speak. This form of Light is what activates and specifies—or determines—the nature of the matter. This splendor or Light exists in all metals as Gold <note>original: *Sol*</note> or Silver <note>original: *Luna*</note>. These are most prominently visible in those two perfect bodies, Gold and Silver, but in other mineral bodies, they are more clouded and eclipsed by an earthly, foul <note>original: *fæculent*</note> obstruction between the brilliance <note>original: *fulgor*</note> and the impurities <note>original: *superfluities*</note>. This obstruction constitutes the imperfection of such bodies; it is accompanied by a rawness and instability <note>original: inconstancy</note> when placed in the fire, where the impure parts carry away the pure.

# An Exposition upon

> *For what are the Sun and Moon <note>original: Sol and Luna</note>, says Avicenna <note>original: Avicen; a famous Persian polymath and alchemist</note>,*
> *But Earth which is pure White and Red?*

So then the Sun and Moon <note>original: Sol and Luna; alchemical terms for Gold and Silver</note> are defined more by their "form" <note>the spiritual or essential nature of a thing</note> than by their "material." For the material itself is a coarse, earthly <note>original: Terrene</note> substance; however, the form of Light that purifies the substance is a most subtle spiritual thing. This Light ennobles the coarseness of the matter through a fire-abiding tincture <term>Tincture: In alchemy, a "tincture" is a spiritualized substance or "medicine" capable of permeating and transforming other materials, specifically by giving them the color and properties of gold or silver</term>.

> *Take from it the said clarity, and then*
> *That Earth will be of little use.*

But if this tincture could be separated from the weight <note>original: *pondus*</note> of the matter, the remainder would be an unprofitable, earthly residue <note>original: Terrestriety</note>. Our work, therefore, is to advance this Light by refining <note>original: exaltation</note> it within the matter. Although in its simple state it exists only as a single unit, it may be brought to a thousandfold power <note>original: Virtue millenary</note>. It can be gradually refined <note>original: exalted</note> so far that the physical matter would seem to be entirely swallowed up by the spiritual form. And yet, in this refinement, it is not the bulk <note>original: *moles*</note> or the weight <note>original: *pondus*</note> that constitutes the power <note>original: Virtue</note> of the Sun or Moon,

but <meta>catchword: but</meta>

<summary>This page explains that the true value of gold and silver (Sol and Luna) lies in their spiritual "form" or "Light" rather than their physical weight, and that the alchemist's goal is to refine this light to a thousandfold power.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the description of the "Philosophic Lead," explaining how the Sun and Moon (Gold and Silver) release their essential qualities into it. It introduces the three alchemical solvents (Menstrues) used in the work.</meta>

...send out their Tinctures <note>Tinctures: The essential, soul-like qualities or colors of the metals</note> into it, which it receives; and, like a fertile soil, it ennobles them a hundred and a hundredfold.

->*These are our Menstrues, both one and all.*<-

Thus, I have given you a general description of our *Menstrues* <term>Menstrues: Solvents or liquid mediums used in alchemy to dissolve and transform solid substances</term>, which are three: *Acetum* (Vinegar), *Elixir*, and *Azoth*. I shall now describe each one particularly.

->*With the first, we naturally and perfectly Calcine bodies.*<-

The first *Menstruum* we call our "sharp Vinegar," along with infinite other names which would be tedious to recite. With this is made our "Magical Solution" of Gold <note>original: *Sol*</note>. This, says Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and chemist</note>, is "the solvent of the world in the sphere of the Moon, purified so many times that it is able to calcine the Sun" <note>original: *Menstruum mundi in sphæra Lunæ toties rectificatum ut possit calcinare Solem*</note>. In this, says the noble author of the *Hermetic Arcanum*, occurs "the eclipse of the Sun and Moon in the Dragon’s Tail" <note>original: *Eclipsis Solis & Lunæ in Cauda Draconis*; this refers to a specific stage of chemical union and darkness in the alchemical process</note>. This is, as Artephius <note>A legendary alchemical figure to whom several medieval texts were attributed</note> says, the only instrument in the world for our Art; for it causes the Sun to putrefy—that is, it causes the metal to lose its hard, solid compaction.

# An Exposition upon

The philosophers call it *Stinking Water* <note>original: "Aqua foetida"</note>, and some call it the *Impurity of Death* <note>original: "Mortis Immundities"</note>. Yet, indeed, it is not unclean in its own nature, but is made pure—as pure as the art of the artist can make it with the help of Nature—by joining like-natured substances together <note>original: "joining Consanguinity with Consanguinity"</note>.

->*Which is usually*<-
->*Named by Philosophers their Green Lion.*<-

This hidden body, or rather **Chaos** <note>Chaos: In alchemy, this refers to the "Materia Prima" or prime matter—the raw, disorganized state of substance before it is purified and perfected</note>, the philosophers have highly praised and deeply concealed. They usually call it their *Green Lion* <note>Green Lion: A famous and multi-layered alchemical symbol; it often represents a powerful solvent or a specific mineral state (like antimony or mercury) capable of "devouring" gold</note>. Many people mistakenly apply this name to *Venus* <note>Venus: The alchemical name for Copper</note>, and some to *Vitriol* <note>Vitriol: A term for various sulfate minerals or sulfuric acid</note>. These are essentially the same thing, as vitriol is merely copper corroded by an undeveloped <note>original: "embryonated"</note> salt. But "fools," says *Ripley* <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), an influential English alchemist whose writings are being commented upon here</note> in his *Errors*, "call it the Green Lion." I shall reveal this subject to you, as far as I dare, in the following song.

<summary>This page explains that the alchemical solvent, though sometimes called "stinking water," is actually a purified substance known as the "Green Lion." The author warns that this "Green Lion" is often misunderstood by "fools" who confuse it with common copper or vitriol.</summary>
<keywords>Aqua foetida, Green Lion, Chaos, Venus, Vitriol, George Ripley, alchemical symbolism, Materia Prima</keywords>

## The Learned

# SAGES' FEAST <note>original: "Sophies Feast"; "Sophy" is an archaic term for a wise man or sage</note>.

Whoever desires to deserve lasting and
eternal fame,
*Learn to tame the **Green Lion**.*
But before you can achieve this through **Art** <note>Art: In this context, "Art" refers to the practice of alchemy, often called the "Great Art" (Ars Magna)</note>,
You must be eager <note>original: "fain"</note> to study him so that you may know him;
Believe me, it is not for a stupid fool,
Nor for someone educated in an ordinary <note>original: "vulgar"</note> school.
I shall therefore portray him vividly,
Lest you leave this banquet empty-handed <note>original: "go lean away"</note>.
I call <note>original: "stile"</note> this song the **Feast of the Learned Sages**,
Prepare yourself to be a worthy guest:
Pay close attention to my words,
Lest you feed on fantasies instead of meat.
This terrible <note>original: "horrid"</note> beast, which we call our Lion,
Has many other names, so that no man shall
Perceive the truth, unless God provides direction,
And shines a light upon his darkened mind.

*Leave behind the false reasoners <note>original: "Sophisters"</note>, and follow my advice:*
*Do not be deluded, for the truth is one;*
*It is not found in many things; this is our Philosopher’s Stone.*
*At first it appears in a defiled <note>meaning: dirty or impure</note> garment,*
*And to speak plainly, it is the Child of Saturn <note>In alchemy, Saturn often represents lead or the raw, heavy starting material—specifically antimony in this context.</note>:*
*His price is low <note>original: "mean"</note>, his venom is very great,*
*His nature <note>original: "constitution"</note> is cold, devoid of heat.*
*Although it is mixed with a sulphur, yet*
*This sulphur is combustible <note>meaning: it can be burned away</note>, to obtain*
*Another metallic <note>original: "Metalline"</note> and pure sulphur,*
*And be sure to mix it with the mercurial part.*
*Look for this sulphur in the House of Aries <note>Aries is the first sign of the zodiac, associated with spring and the metal iron.</note>;*

*To Colchis <note>original: "Colchos," the destination of the Argonauts to find the Golden Fleece</note>; this is what no book*
*Has yet revealed, and yet I will proceed,*
*And quickly unfold greater mysteries.*
*Our subject is in no way malleable <note>meaning: it cannot be hammered or shaped like soft metal</note>;*
*It is metallic, and its color is black <note>original: "sable"</note>,*
*With intermixed silver <note>original: "Argent"</note>, which in veins*
*Colors <note>original: "stains"</note> the black field with glittering branches.*

...kill the **Eagles** <note>In alchemy, "killing the eagles" refers to fixing a volatile substance—stopping it from evaporating or sublimating—so that it remains stable in the heat.</note>; and out of these tiny particles <note>original: "Atoms"</note> the second Water shall be revealed through dissolution.

->*With the third humidity, which is most permanent.*<-

The third **solvent** <note>original: "Menstrue"</note> is called the "second Water" by **Artephius** <note>Artephius (c. 1150) was a legendary alchemical author whose works were highly influential in the medieval and Renaissance periods.</note>. He calls it this because he joins our second solvent together with the first; however, in the places where he describes the "three fires" in detail, he then distinguishes three separate solvents <note>original: "Menstruums"</note>.

Many **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers specifically to alchemists or practitioners of the "Hermetic Philosophy."</note> have followed a similar course in the description of their operations. Some omit the first solvent, or at least confuse it with the second, to further hide the secrets of the **Art** <note>The "Art" is a common term for alchemy or the "Great Work."</note>.

But we have (beyond what anyone has performed until now) particularly insisted upon explaining the three in their proper order. We have taken more pains in the discovery of the first because the wise **Ancients** took such great care to conceal that one the most. After that, we proceeded in an orderly way to the second solvent, which we handled in a similar way. Now that this is performed, we turn our attention to the third.

This is called by *Ripley* <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was one of the most famous English alchemists, best known for his work *The Compound of Alchymy*.</note> a most permanent Humidity. Note, by the way, that the "first Water" is also called a "permanent Water" by various authors; however, take notice that there is a different reason for each name. 

First of all, all *Mercury* is "permanent Water"—that is, its parts have no heterogeneity <note>original: "Heterogeneity"; meaning the substance is uniform or composed of parts of the same kind.</note>. Its parts will not leave one another during an examination by fire; instead, either the whole substance flies off and is unstable <note>original: "unconstant"</note>, or else the whole substance remains and is stable <note>original: "constant"</note> in the trial of *Vulcan* <note>Vulcan was the Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this refers to the heat of the furnace.</note>. Our first *solvent* <note>original: "Menstruum"</note> behaves in this same way. 

In this regard, our *Mercury* and *Common Mercury* agree, besides sharing the same material identity; for it is only the form that distinguishes them. But furthermore, our Water is "permanent" with the "Body," which *Common Mercury* is not. This means that through digestion <note>In alchemy, digestion is the process of applying gentle, continuous heat to a substance over a long period to mature it.</note>, it unites with the Body rather than just clinging to it, so that both together form a single *undivided entity* <note>original: "Individuum"</note>. This is achieved through our secret Conjunction <note>A "conjunction" is the union of two substances, often described as a chemical marriage between different principles.</note>. 

Lastly, when the Body is reduced in this way by our Water, the four Elements are ultimately united within this Water. After Putrefaction <note>The stage of the "Great Work" where the matter turns black and decomposes, often called the "nigredo."</note> and Purification, we reach the final, most praiseworthy fourfold union <note>original: "Tetraptive Conjunction"; referring to the balancing and joining of the four classical elements: earth, air, fire, and water.</note>,

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was a famous English alchemist and Augustinian canon whose works were heavily studied and commented upon in the 17th century.</note>

all *Sulphur*, and entirely incombustible. This work is called the burning of *Hermes'* Tree to ashes, which is done three times. First, it is turned into a black, oily <note>original: "unctuous"</note> <term>Calx</term> <note>A powder or crumbly residue produced by heating a mineral or metal to high temperatures.</note> as fine as tiny particles <note>original: "Atoms"</note> that can only be seen in sunbeams. Second, it becomes a fine white <term>Calx</term>, in which the *Moon* is at the full. The third is a red <term>Calx</term>, in which the *Sun* is rising <note>original: "Orient"</note>.

Now, understand that the first <term>Calcination</term> <note>The process of reducing a substance to a powder through heat.</note> comes from the power of the *Sun*. In this stage, the *Sun* seeks to rise; however, because of the equal opposition it finds from the water, it is obscured by clouds. Afterward, through the Earth coming between them <note>original: "interposition of the Earth"</note>, it is totally eclipsed.

Therefore, because this fire is mixed with the natural fire of the *Sun* <note>original Latin: "Sol"</note>—which is dissolved within it—it is called "unnatural." The first fire of our Water is called "Fire against nature," and the fire of the *Sulphur* of the "perfect Body" is called the "Fire of nature."

the moistening of our Water, it immediately <note>original: "straight"</note> begins to follow the Spirit upon the Fire. To show its union to the Body, this Soul rises no higher than its root or base <note>original: "Basis"</note> below can allow; and to demonstrate its love for the Spirit, it climbs after it higher and higher for its duration <note>original: "season"</note>, until at last it returns to where it came from.

### The Comparison of the Tree

This Tree of ours some have compared to one thing, and some to another; some to a Cypress or Fir tree, which indeed may seem to resemble it. Others have compared it to hawthorn trees, as *Ripley* does in his *Gate of Cibation* <note>original: "Gate of Cibation"; the seventh stage of George Ripley’s alchemical process, where the substance is "fed" with liquid to sustain its growth</note>. Others compare it to shrubs and bushes, or to thick woods; and in these woods, says *Lambspring* <note>original: "Lambsprint"; refers to the author of *The Book of Lambspring*, a famous alchemical emblem book</note>, there is a beast that is black all over. 

I confess there is a similarity between our sprouting <note>original: "Germination"</note> and all of these. Others, because of the moisture <note>original: "Humidity"</note> of the mixture <note>original: "Compound"</note>—which is repeatedly returning in drops—have likened it to a marshy, low-lying bog in which rushes grow and toads live. 

Others have called it their Coral, which is indeed the most fitting comparison. For in our Tree, there are shoots and sprigs without anything that can be properly compared to leaves. Just as coral is a union of a vegetable and a stony nature, so it is in our Tree (for stones and minerals share a similar composition). Our Tree is metallic <note>original: "Metalline"</note>, and yet through the

power of God, it seems to grow like a plant <note>original: "Vegetate"</note>. 

Secondly, coral grows underwater, where one would think no plant could grow; our Tree likewise grows in a heat so intense that no other plant except itself could survive. 

Thirdly, coral has many sprigs and branches but no leaves; our Tree is the same. 

Fourthly, coral, while it is underwater, has a very sharp and stinging <note>original: "exquisite biting"</note> taste, which it quickly loses when exposed to the air. Similarly, our Stone—or Metallic Tree—has a sharp and fiery <note>original: "pontique Fiery"</note> nature while in its proper environment, but once removed, it loses this quality forever and cannot recover it. 

Fifthly, there are five kinds of coral: the common gray, the milk-white, the green, the blood-red, and the black. Our Tree, during its different stages <note>original: "periods"</note>, takes on all of these colors. In this form, the Tree is dried by the heat of the fire into a <term>calx</term> <note>a powdery metallic oxide produced by heating</note>, which is called the *Ashes of Hermes' Tree*. 

Lastly, coral is heavier than any other plant; similarly, our Tree is heavier than all plants—and even heavier than coral itself—in its weight <note>original: "ponderosity"</note>. This *Mercury*, thus renovated or newborn, may be handled in different ways by the Philosopher. *Mercury* is called "Vegetable" <note>In alchemy, "vegetable" refers to the principle of growth, life, and movement, rather than just a plant.</note> because it flies and returns, and causes the Earth to sprout and bud with tender branches. These two—the base oil or *Sulphur*, and the vegetable solvent <term>Menstruum</term> <note>original: "Menstrue"; a liquid used to dissolve or extract substances</note>—at last form one entire, indivisible substance, which possesses an <term>hermaphroditic</term> <note>Combining both male (Sulphur/Hot) and female (Mercury/Cold) alchemical principles.</note> quality: the Man is the Woman, and the Woman is the Man; the *Sulphur* is the *Mercury*, and the *Mercury* is the *Sulphur*. In this respect, the Earth flies as if it were Heaven, and eventually, the Heaven will <term>precipitate</term> <note>original: "precipitate"; to fall or descend from a higher state into a solid form</note> in the form of Earth. Then our work is at an end; that is the "Harvest in the South," when the glorified Soul has become a spiritual Body, which is called our Stone of Paradise.

> *Of a nature so fiery that we call it*
> *Our **Basilisk**, or our **Cockatrice**,* <note>The Basilisk and Cockatrice were mythical serpents whose gaze was said to be lethal; in alchemy, they represent the Stone’s swift power to "kill" or fix mercury.</note>

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

The **Essential Mercury** of our **Stone**, which is our **first Water**, I shall leave unrevealed <note>original: "undiscovered"</note>. Otherwise, if it could be explained without the danger of exposing <note>original: "prostituting"</note> the secret to unworthy people, I would proclaim it with trumpets for the good of mankind.

*As Raymund <note>Raymund Lully (c. 1232–1315), a major figure in medieval alchemy and logic.</note> said when he was old,*
*It would be worth much more than gold.*

This **Oil** or **Quintessence** <term>Quintessence</term><note>The "fifth essence" or purest form of a substance, believed to have extraordinary healing or transformative powers.</note>, this **Balsam of Health**, is something we prize for its wonderful virtues. We value it not just because it allows one to avoid the great snare of poverty, but for its magnificent power against all diseases of both body and mind. This is testified to by Lully, Trevisan <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist famous for his long search for the philosopher's stone.</note>, Artephius <note>A legendary alchemical writer, often said to have lived over a thousand years using alchemical secrets.</note>, and that excellent book titled *The Golden Hour* <note>original Latin: "Aurea Hora"</note> or *The Rising Dawn* <note>original Latin: "Consurgens Aurora"</note>.

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

The **essential mercury** <term>Essential Mercury</term><note>In alchemy, this refers to a purified, philosophical form of mercury that serves as the "soul" or living principle of the metal, essential for creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> of our stone, which is our **first water** <term>First Water</term><note>The primary solvent or liquid medium used at the start of the alchemical process to dissolve the base materials.</note>, I shall leave hidden <note>original: "undiscovered"</note>. Otherwise—if it could be explained without the danger of exposing <note>original: "prostituting"</note> the other secret to unworthy people—I would proclaim it with trumpets for the good of mankind.

*Which Raymond said when he was old,*
*Would be worth much more than gold.* <note>original: "Much more then Gold would stand in stead." A reference to Raymond Lully, a famous medieval philosopher and alleged alchemist.</note>

We value this **oil** or **quintessence** <term>Quintessence</term><note>Literally the "fifth essence," representing the purest and most potent extract of a substance, beyond the four physical elements.</note>—this **balsam of health**—for its wonderful power <note>original: "virtue"</note>. We prize it not merely because it allows us to avoid poverty—that great trap <note>original: "snare"</note>—but for its wonderful, prevailing excellence against all diseases of both body and mind. This is confirmed by the writings of **Lully** <note>Raymond Lully (c. 1232–1315), whose name was attached to many influential alchemical texts.</note>, **Trevisan** <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), an alchemist who famously spent his life and fortune seeking the Stone.</note>, and **Artephius** <note>A legendary alchemical figure, allegedly a 12th-century writer who claimed to have lived for a thousand years thanks to the elixir.</note>, along with that excellent book entitled *The Golden Hour* <note>original Latin: *Aurea Hora*</note>, or *The Rising Dawn* <note>original Latin: *Consurgens Aurora*; a famous 15th-century alchemical manuscript known for its rich allegorical illustrations.</note>.

## An Exposition upon Sir George Ripley

> *For when he was near death from old age,*
> *He made from it drinkable gold <note>original: "Aurum Potabile"</note>,*
> *Which revived him, as people could see.*

By the power of this <term>quintessence</term> <note>Literally the "fifth essence," representing the purest and most potent extract of a substance, beyond the four physical elements.</note>, **Artephius** <note>A legendary alchemist who, according to tradition, discovered the secret of long life and lived for over a thousand years.</note> testifies that he lived for more than a thousand years. **Flammel** <note>Nicolas Flammel (c. 1330–1418), a French scrivener who posthumously gained a reputation as a successful alchemist who discovered the Philosopher’s Stone.</note> also records of it that it triumphs over all the miseries of the world. **Johannes de Laznioro** <note>John of Laznioro, a 15th-century alchemist known for his writings on the medicinal properties of the Stone.</note> is even bolder and says that if a man should taste even a single grain of it while in the agony of death, every deadly plague <note>original: "mortal Pestilence"</note> would leave him.

Since this knowledge is so incredibly profitable—being the very mirror <note>original: "Looking-glass"</note> of Nature, the antidote to poverty and sickness, and consequently the destroyer <note>original: "Cut-throat"</note> of greed <note>original: "Covetousness"</note>, pride, ambition, and similar shameful passions—who would not devote a little time to investigating it? In this matter, let me assure you in the exact words of **Trevisan** <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous alchemist who emphasized that the process was simpler than most people imagined.</note>: the art is so simple that, if it were described plainly <note>original: "nakedly"</note>, it would be looked down upon <note>original: "contemptible"</note>. And yet in common trades <note>original: "vulgar Mechanics"</note>, how common is it for men to serve seven,

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

...eight, yes, even ten years of apprenticeship to master them? And for some who are a bit more talented <note>original: "ingenious"</note>, how often are significant sums of money paid in addition <note>original: "to boot"</note>? Yet in this noble Art—which so far exceeds all human sciences as the Sun exceeds a candle—who will apply themselves with diligence? Unless it be some money-minded fools <note>original: "Sots"</note> who seek only wealth; and yet how quickly even they grow weary! 

So it is that few or none persist in their inquiry, except for a few roguish tricksters <note>original: "Sophisters"</note> who live by deceiving others <note>original: "Cozening"</note>. Because of such people, the Art is disgraced and spoken of poorly. Yet trust me (for I speak from experience), the Art is both true and easy. Indeed, it is so easy that if you were to see the experiment performed, you would not believe it. I did not make five wrong experiments in it before I discovered the truth, although in some specific manual operations <note>original: "Encheiresis," from the Greek for "handiwork" or "practical method"</note> I erred often. Yet even in my errors, I knew I was a master, and in less than two full years and a half, I went from being an unlearned layman <note>original: "vulgar Ignoramus"</note> to a true <term>Adept</term> <note>In alchemy, an Adept is someone who has successfully discovered the secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. 

I now possess the Secret through the goodness of God. And that this is true, there are those still alive who can bear

# CALCINATION.

### Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

...spies; but we will knock for a guide who may go along with us. For know that this castle is a garrison and must not be viewed without a guide who may conduct us in and out, and show us the places through which we pass. 

And now that he has come, I shall acquaint you somewhat with his temperament <note>original: "conditions"</note>, so that you may know how to please him. This will make him more willing to accompany you on the right path and not leave you—as he has done to some—nor mislead you—as he has done to others. Even those who have attempted this work with great success in the knowledge of the required materials have nonetheless fatally erred by not knowing how to please their guide. He has a temperament <note>original: "humour"</note> of his own that is unequaled in the world; and if you make him either sullen or angry <note>original: "choleric"</note>, you might as well give up the enterprise.

First of all, then, know that in his nature <note>original: "for his parts"</note> he is a very stupid fool; there is no one more simple among all his brothers. Yet he is most faithful to his Lord and does all things for him most

prudently, ordering all things in the family very discreetly. I attribute this more to a natural instinct than to any sharpness of mind <note>original: "quickness of parts"</note>. He is very faithful; for that reason, he will never ask or answer any questions but proceeds silently. Nor will he ever go before you, but will follow instead. You must be very cautious how you lead him; if he finds an opportunity, he will escape <note>original: "give you the flip"</note> and leave you to a world of misfortune.

You can tell by his facial expression <note>original: "countenance"</note> whether he is pleased or displeased. Therefore, place him in bonds—that is, seal him tightly where he cannot escape—then proceed wisely with heat, and constantly observe his face as he follows. You will recognize his anger by a redness in his face, and his sullenness by his sluggish <note>original: "lumpish"</note> behavior. When in a good mood, he is reasonably <note>original: "indifferent"</note> active and cheerful. Thus, you shall move forward, turn, or go back, as you see his expression and temperament shift. 

Next, you must understand that he was born to be a drudge <term>drudge: someone who performs hard, menial, or monotonous work</term> and is the very servant to all his brothers. would monopolize the whole toil and task of labor for himself; and if you attempt to do anything yourself, he will immediately take offense <note>original: "take snuff"</note> and leave all the work for you to do, refusing to do another stroke more.

Thirdly, through long custom, he has acquired a habit of perpetual working. Therefore, if you allow him even one hour’s rest, he will never work again. For in his father’s house, he committed the offense of *Ham* <note>original: "Cham"; in the Bible, Ham was the son of Noah whose descendants were cursed to servitude</note> and is therefore judged to be a servant of servants. These are the general characteristics of your Guide, and you must address yourself to him accordingly, or else your labor will be in vain.

Since you see me naked with this King, know that this place and my kingdom are in the state of innocence, even though we have been made subject to vanity <note>original: "Vanity"; likely referring to the biblical concept of the "vanity of the world" or the transitory nature of physical life after the Fall.</note> by the Fall of Adam. Until the final restoration from that Fall, I am forbidden to do anything of my own accord that goes beyond the state of fading corruption—even though all things possess an incorruptible spirit. When heaven and earth are renewed, that spirit shall bring about an unchanging glory in all these things. 

Know then that this King is my servant, and he has many brothers who were taken prisoner and kept in bondage while trying to reach him. There is no way to redeem them unless he gives his own flesh and blood as their ransom. However, this ransom cannot be effective unless he dies and rises from the dead. 

I cannot perform this by myself, nor can anyone help me in this except for man alone. He has also given man the free power to act as His subordinate in the world, although through

darkness, but daylight about me. I wondered at the very strange change and still felt for my thread, which by that light I could not see, but only feel. I began to look about me to see where I was, and behold, I was in a ruinous place with millions of turnings, each leading in different directions. Every room was so enlightened with "fox-fire" <note>fox-fire: a natural glow produced by fungi on decaying wood</note> and the tails of glow-worms that, for want of a better light, it seemed as if it were day. 

I took out of my pocket a small book to see if I could read it. It was titled *The Handbook of Restored Physics* <note>original Latin: "Enchiridion Physicæ Restitutæ," a work by the alchemist Jean d'Espagnet first published in 1623</note>, with a "Secret" <note>original Latin: "Arcanum"—a secret, mystery, or specialized alchemical remedy</note> at the end of it, but I could not read one word in it. 

I was met by a man, aged and decrepit; his face was rugged, his eyes bleared, and his hands and fingers were worn away <note>original: "corraded"—eroded or eaten away, perhaps by the corrosive chemicals used in alchemical experiments</note>. ### *With Sulphurs.*

I found one furnace most skillfully <note>original: "curiously"</note> built, in which all degrees of heat imaginable by human art might be maintained with a single fire. In this furnace was set a multitude of intricate glass vessels containing several substances <note>original: "Matters"</note>; some were digesting <note>term: slow heating to promote decomposition or maturation</note>, others subliming <note>term: heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note>, others distilling, and others calcining <note>term: heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to powder or drive off volatile parts</note>. Around the glasses and the furnace was written this quote from **Geber** <note>Geber: The Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century Islamic polymath often called the "Father of Chemistry."</note>: "By God, **Sulphur** is all that light-giving thing which is above the earth" <note>original Latin: *Per Deum Sulphur est omne illud illuminans quod est supra terram.*</note>.

By this I knew that **Sulphur** <note>In alchemy, Sulphur is one of the "Tria Prima" (Three Primes), representing the active, masculine principle of fire and the soul.</note> was the subject being worked upon, and indeed with such great skill <note>original: "cunning"</note> that I could not help but admire the ingenuity of the men. Knowing that what a person prizes (even if it is a trifle), to spoil or destroy it would be an injury, I did not meddle with any glass vessel. Indeed, there were laborers there who did not flee because they knew nothing; they only worked as they were directed. When I arrived, they could not perceive that I had a candle in my hand, and they wondered [that] their masters

I asked to know the reason for their error so that I might know how to avoid doing the same.

> *For by such calcination their bodies are ruined <note>original: "shent"</note>,*
> *Which diminishes the moisture of our Stone;*
> *Therefore, when bodies are burnt <note>original: "brent"</note> to powder,*
> *As dry as the ashes of a tree or bone,*

Then she said: "Beyond the fact that they do not work with the true Matter, they do not work in the right way either. These are two dire errors. Our work is to create a substance that is fluid, penetrating, and capable of entering into—and merging with—imperfect metals. For this reason, we preserve humidity; without it, our Stone cannot be penetrative. 

So, instead of purifying what is crude and ripening what is raw through these calcinations, the delicate Soul <note>In alchemy, the "soul" refers to the volatile, essential essence of a substance</note> is driven away <note>original: "put to flight"</note>, and the raw parts are only more strongly turned to glass <note>original: "vitrified"</note>. Because of this, all hope of success is completely lost. Take this as a rule: whatever is produced by the violence of fire..."

...or corrosives, and is turned into a dry powder or calx <term>calx: the powdery residue or "ash" left after a metal or mineral has been heated or chemically broken down</term>, it is completely rejected <note>original: "reprobate"</note> in our work. For though we perform calcination, it is done in such a fire that our moisture is not burnt away, and in a vessel so tightly closed that the spirits are kept inside. In short, our process <term>regimen: a prescribed course of chemical treatment or heating</term> is so gentle regarding our Matter, that the moisture is increased and made more oily <note>original: "unctuous"</note>, and consequently more able to penetrate <note>original: "ingressive"</note> other substances.

> *And for a sure foundation of our true calcination,*
> *Work wisely, joining only like with like,*
> *For like has a natural attraction <note>original: "appetitive inclination"</note> toward its own kind.*

But all this is not enough to explain our calcination, for the actual operation follows the preparation. He who does not prepare his materials before beginning to work, and does not set the true active and passive elements <term>Agent and Patient: the active, "masculine" principle and the passive, "feminine" material in an alchemical reaction</term> together, will find that his process cannot and will not produce anything. Therefore, you must first know that we join like with like in our work, for Nature is improved and preserved by its own Nature. <page-num>135</page-num>

### Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

...knowing the causes of things, so that every puff of sophisters <term>Sophister: a person who uses clever but false arguments, particularly in a deceptive way</term> will toss you around like a feather is tossed in the air by a blast of wind. For our books are full of obscurity, and philosophers write terrible metaphors and riddles for those who do not have a firm foundation. Like a rushing stream, these riddles will carry them headlong into despair and errors that they can never escape until they understand our writings well enough to discern the true subject matter of our secrets. > Join like with like <note>original: "kind with kind"</note>, as is only reasonable,
> For every bud <note>original: "Burgeon"</note> corresponds to its own seed;

> Each thing is first calcined <term>Calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder, often the first step in the alchemical process</term> within its own kind.
> If you understand this well, you will find fruit in it.

Step, therefore, not one step further until you have learned this lesson: namely, to wed blood relations with blood relations <note>original: "consanguinity"</note>.

# An Exposition upon

[Page 136]

...consanguinity <note>original: "guinity," completing the word from the previous page</note>, and consider well what it is you desire to produce; let that be your intention. Take the final goal of your intention as the first step of your principles. This follows the path of Nature and is the true basis of all creation <note>original: "Generation"</note>, for nothing creates anything outside of its own kind. A man fathers a man and not a lion, nor does a lion father a sheep; a rose does not produce a thorn, nor does a nettle produce a carnation <note>original: "Gilliflower"</note>. 

If it were necessary, I could demonstrate this specifically across all plant <note>original: "Vegetable"</note>, animal, and mineral bodies, but it is so obvious a thing that I need say no more. I leave it with you as the foundation stone upon which you shall build whatever you intend. Attempt nothing outside of its own nature and kind, or you will harvest a fantasy instead of the truth. 

Whatever you intend to increase by way of multiplication <term>Multiplication: the alchemical process of increasing the power, quantity, or "virtue" of the Philosopher's Stone</term>, attempt it only within its own kind. This is especially true in this work, in which calcination <term>Calcination: the first stage of the alchemical process; here described as the mingling of the "seeds" of substances</term> is the mingling of seminal <note>original: "Seminal influence" (referring to the "seed" or life-force within a substance)</note> influence, which must be done through homogeneity <note>original: "homogeneity" (the state of being of the same kind or nature)</note>. If you correctly understand the cause of this, and know how...

# Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

[Page 137]

...how to apply this doctrine in your work <note>original: "operation"</note> as you should. In doing so, you will find great benefit, and a door will be opened to the discovery of greater mysteries.

->*And we make the calcined substance oily, black, white, and red.*<-
<term>Calx: In traditional chemistry, a "calx" is a dry powder left after heating a metal (an oxide). In this alchemical context, however, the author specifies that their "Magical Calx" is not a dry powder but an "unctuous" or oily substance.</term>

Now the opportunity of this discussion leads me to treat our "Magical Calxes." Know that we calcine three times in our work. The general principle is that our calxes are not burnt <note>original: "combust"</note> powders, but are oily <note>original: "unctuous"</note>. In these, we ennoble the *Sulphurs*, which are the basis of the Radical Humidity <term>Radical Humidity: the fundamental, life-giving moisture believed to be inherent in all matter; the "liquid" aspect of the soul of the metal</term> that *Raymond* <note>original: "Raymund" (referring to the famous alchemist Raymond Lully)</note> calls oils and ointments <note>original: "Unguents"</note>.

Therefore, our calxes are all of one kind and only differ in their color and stage of maturity. Our first is the blackest of blacks and is called *Saturn* <note>Saturn traditionally represents lead or the "Nigredo" (blackening) stage, the first phase of the alchemical Great Work</note>; our second is the whitest of whites and is called *Luna* <note>Luna represents silver or the "Albedo" (whitening) stage</note>; our third is the reddest of reds and is called *Sol* <note>Sol represents gold or the final "Rubedo" (reddening) stage</note>.

<summary>This page explains that the alchemist's "calx" is a moist, oily substance rather than a dry powder, and it describes the three stages of its transformation through the colors black (Saturn), white (Luna), and red (Sol).</summary>

...consanguinity <note>original: "guinity," completing the word "consanguinity" from the previous page, meaning relationship by descent from the same ancestor; in alchemy, this refers to using substances of the same nature</note>, and consider carefully what it is you desire to produce. This follows the laws of Nature and is the true basis of all generation, for nothing reproduces outside of its own kind. 

A man begets a man, not a lion; nor does a lion beget a sheep. A rose does not produce a thorn, nor does a nettle produce a gillyflower <note>original: "Gilliflower," a term for clove-scented flowers like carnations or wallflowers</note>. If it were necessary, I could demonstrate this specifically across all plant <note>original: "Vegetable"</note>, animal, and mineral bodies; however, it is so obvious that I need say no more. Attempt nothing outside of its own nature and kind, lest you reap a fantasy instead of the truth. This is especially true in this work, where calcination is the mingling of seminal influence, which must be done through homogeneity <note>original: "homogeneity," meaning substances of the same kind or nature</note>. ### Concerning the three degrees before our base is perfect.

These residues <note>original: "Calxes," the solid substances remaining after a material has been heated or "calcined"</note> are the end points of several circulations <note>repeated cycles of heating, evaporation, and condensation within a sealed vessel</note>, and a specific number of degrees are assigned to each of them. Our first circulation mingles the elements; our second renews the air; and the third exalts the fire. At that point, our Stone is perfect.

*Flexible as wax, otherwise they are of no value.*

Our residues <note>original: "Calxes"</note>, having been refined through these stages, are distinguishable from all other residues in the world. First, they are produced without any manual intervention <note>original: "laying on of hands"; this refers to the alchemical concept of the "work without hands," where nature and heat perform the transformation inside a sealed glass without the chemist's physical touch</note>. Second, they are distinguished by the exquisite refinement of their particles—for they are finer than the dust motes seen in a sunbeam <note>original: "Atoms of the Sun"</note>—joined with an exceptional moisture <note>original: "Humidity"</note>. Because of this moisture, and to the astonishment of those who see it, they flow like melted wax. The first residue flows in the glass vessel through the continued application of heat, and the second does the same, as it has not yet reached its final period of perfection. Both the second and third residues will flow when placed upon a plate of hot metal, or upon a melted metal, or on
->Mercury<-

Know also that our Water is not called the *Moon* except during, or in reference to, the conjunction <term>conjunction</term> <note>The union of the male and female, or fixed and volatile, principles</note>, and in that context, let the *Moon* be two. In reference to its own constitution, it is called *Mercury* (for it must be in that form and flow), and so it ought to be four parts to the *Sun*. That is seven Eagles, which are not added to the *Sun* before the count of three; by adding four more, you reach seven.

### Sir George Ripley's First Gate

...even when you desire to rest <note>original: "to wink," meaning to sleep or turn a blind eye to the work</note>, it will cost you more persistent boiling to dry up three parts of Water than two. There must necessarily be a difference in the internal **weight** <term>pondus</term> <note>original: pondus; the specific alchemical weight or proportion of the substances</note>; for if the Water has the same internal heat and the external fire remains the same, the difference in the **boiling process** <note>original: decoction</note> between two parts and three will be nearly twice as long until the stage of "blackness" is reached. However, after the blackness appears, the time required for both is the same. Yet either of these proportions is correct, provided you are careful to adjust your **Mercury** <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the volatile, feminine, and "watery" principle of a substance.</note> in heat, and manage the **operation** <note>original: Regimen</note> of your furnace according to which of these proportions you use. > *If the Water is also equal in proportion*
> *To the Earth, with heat in due measure,*
> *From them will grow a new sprout,*
> *Both white and red in a pure tincture,*
> *Which in the fire shall endure forever.*
> *Kill the living, and the dead revive;*
> *Make the Trinity a Unity without any struggle.*
> *This is the surest and best proportion.*

There is another external proportion, which is three parts of the **Spirit** <note>In alchemy, the "Spirit" is the volatile, feminine, or watery principle (Mercury).</note> to one part of the **Body** <note>The "Body" is the solid, masculine, or earthy principle (Sulfur/Earth).</note>, according to the work of the noble **Bacon** <note>Likely Roger Bacon (c. 1219–1292), a medieval philosopher and scientist whose works were central to the alchemical tradition.</note> and many others. I am familiar with both, but I will not set down the foundations of this second method here. If you understand the first method, its rules should guide you in the second; for there is an infallible rule of proportion regarding how the external and internal elements must work together to create a sweet harmony. Only let me assure you of this, quoting **Norton** <note>Thomas Norton (c. 1433–1513), an English alchemist and author of 'The Ordinal of Alchemy.'</note>: *“That if your Body has plenty of drink, then you must stay awake...”*

### An Exposition upon

*Dry up your moisture with the most temperate heat.*
*Assist dissolution with the moisture of the Moon,* <note>original: "moisture of the Moon"; in alchemy, the Moon often represents silver or the cold, moist, feminine principle</note>
*And coagulation <note>original: "Congelation"; the process of thickening or solidifying</note> with the Sun, then you are finished.*

When you have done this, be sure to heat <note>original: "decoct"</note> it over a very gentle fire until it is dry. This dryness should be achieved not by evaporating <note>original: "exhaling"</note> the water, but by thickening <note>original: "coagulating"</note> it into the solid substance <note>original: "Body"</note>. In this, your main concern must be that your vessel is sealed <note>original: "close"</note> and your fire is gentle. Now, the way to distinguish a gentle fire from a violent one is a secret deeply hidden by the "envious" <note>original: "envious"; a term used for alchemical authors who intentionally obscured their writings to keep secrets from the uninitiated</note>; however, I shall prescribe a few rules:

1. Know that it is the internal fire of the <term>Sulphur</term> <note>in alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle within a substance</note> within your water which performs the entire work.

2. The external fire is only an outward condition, yet it is so absolutely necessary that nothing can be achieved without it.

## Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

...subliming <note>original: "subliming"; the process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used for purification</note> the thin part, and so separating both gently and with skill <note>original: "suaviter & cum ingenio"</note> according to the ancient Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary mythical founder of alchemy</note>.

4. All of our Great Work <note>original: "Mastery"</note> consists in vapor, which cannot be achieved without sublimation and distillation. For if our spirit did not ascend in a living form, it would all rise and remain stuck; but by ascending quickly, it returns again and moistens the "Body" <note>the solid material remaining at the bottom of the vessel</note>.

5. Our distillation or circulation is not without a constant motion of separation; for just as the subtle part is separated from the coarse <note>original: "gross"</note> by sublimation, so the thick part of what is below is separated from the thin. This is done by continual boiling and heating <note>original: "decocting"</note>, without a moment’s interruption. Therefore, says Hermes, "you shall separate the subtle from the coarse, and the thick from the thin."

6. Our tender spirit learns every day to endure fire more and more. Therefore, says Arnold <note>Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a famous alchemist and physician</note>, boil it with a fire that increases daily. Yet it is only one degree of fire, and that is boiling, until the "Woman’s Empire" <note>the phase of the process dominated by moisture and the "feminine" principle, often associated with the color white</note> is defeated; and then there is another degree, and that is roasting.

<header>An Exposition upon</header>

...never so full of spirit as it was before.

21. Yet some of it will be lost in a short heating <note>original: "decoction"; a process of boiling or heating to extract essences or transform a substance</note>, and most of it will be gone within thirty days. In a fire managed according to these rules, dry up your moisture. To dry up the moisture of your "Water," you must dissolve the structure <note>original: "Compages"; the framework or internal assembly of a solid body</note> of your "Body." Then your Water dissolves your Body, and your dissolved Body solidifies <note>original: "re-congeals"</note> itself through further heating. In doing so, it hardens the Water, which was so closely united with it during dissolution that they became one.

> ->*Four natures into a fifth so shall you turn,*<-
> ->*Which is a nature most perfect and balanced.*<-

In this way, you shall turn your four qualities (which were in conflict <note>original: "repugnancy"</note>) into a fifth that is balanced <note>original: "temperate"; refers to the "Quintessence" or a state where the four elements are perfectly harmonized</note>. That is to say, in this dryness of the heating process <note>original: "Calcination"; the reduction of a substance to a dry powder through intense heat</note>, you shall reconcile the ***Mercury*** (with its qualities of cold and moisture) to the ***Sulphur*** (with its qualities of heat and dryness). <page-num>163</page-num>
# Sir George Ripley’s First Gate.

Gold would have to penetrate its own dimensions so many times to satisfy your foolish <note>original: "fond"; in this period, "fond" often meant "foolish" or "deluded"</note> desires. What you are attempting is to force Nature and to create seeds <note>original: "Sperms"; the reproductive essence or "germ" of a substance</note>, both of which are futile to undertake and truly impossible. Abandon then this false reasoning <note>original: "Sophistry"; deceptive or fallacious arguments used by "false" alchemists</note> and embrace the true light. 

To create seeds is the property of God alone, and everything has its own seed, just as it has its own form. There is nothing that has a reproductive power <note>original: "seminal virtue"</note> applicable to two different things; everything has its own seed according to its own nature <note>original: "Form"</note>. Therefore, if gold and silver are your goal, let those same metals be the materials you work upon. Gold is your first foundation, for your "white" stage <note>the *Albedo*, or white stone, which can transmute metals into silver</note> must first come out of your "red" <note>the *Rubedo*, or red stone, associated with gold. Ripley suggests that the essence of gold is required even to reach the silver stage</note>. When your White Stone is perfect, then you may use common silver <note>original: "*Luna vulgar*"; ordinary silver as opposed to the "Philosophical Silver" created during the Work</note>. 

Now, the hidden seed of gold must be extracted, and that is done by mercury <note>original: "*Mercury*"; specifically "Philosophical Mercury," the specialized alchemical solvent</note> during the process of calcination <note>the process of heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or change its chemical state</note>. This operation is the "Mineral Copulation," in which the seeds are released and mixed together. Then the old body shall die, and a new body shall rise again, endowed with the power to multiply <note>original: "multiplicative virtue"; the ability of the finished Philosopher's Stone to transform a large quantity of base metal into gold</note>, according to the nature of all things; for—

<page-num>164</page-num>

It reeks of absurdity and ignorance to allow almost all things a power of multiplication while denying it to Gold, the most perfect of all earthly <note>original: "sublunary"; literally "under the moon," referring to the physical world subject to change and decay</note> things.

*And if it were true that profit could be found,*
*In things which are not metallic <note>original: "Metalline"</note>.*

But some will say: "How will you answer the Philosophers who affirm that their Stone is in all things—though in some things it is nearer and in others at a greater distance—yet in all things according to the rule of being 'nearer and farther' <note>original Latin: *propinquius & remotius*</note>?" To such people I answer: I grant and know that all things originally owe all their primary material to Water, and their form to Light. According to the union <note>original: "congress"</note> of these two principles, through the command of the Architect <note>the Creator or God</note>, this Light illuminates the material Water in a unique way, according to the ideal forms <note>original: "species"</note> which existed previously in the Archetype <note>the original model or the mind of God</note>. So then, the Matter resides in Water, the shaping power <note>original: "Informing"</note> resides in Light, and the determination of the Form—which is, so to speak, the essential nature of the form—resides in the will of the Creator.

<meta>This page continues the author's metaphysical explanation of creation, moving from the general principles of Water and Light to the specific "sealing" or fixing of distinct biological and material categories by divine command.</meta>

<page-num>165</page-num>
<header>Sir George Ripley’s First Gate <note>Sir George Ripley was a famous 15th-century English alchemist; "First Gate" refers to Calcination, the first of twelve stages in his alchemical process.</note></header>

The Creator first imprinted or "sealed" this in the word *fiat* <note>original Latin: "let it be done"</note> and confirmed it in His command: *let each thing bring forth according to its kind* <note>original Latin: *producat unumquodque juxta speciem suam*</note>. Now, to apply this to our present purpose: while all things are the same in their basic components of Water and Light, they differ in how that Light is specifically defined <note>original: "determination of Illumination"</note>. This defining decree of the Almighty established a great variety in what Matter and Form—which are general in themselves—could produce. 

Because these categories were thus fixed <note>original: "sealed"</note>, nothing can pass from its own kind to mix with another kind without creating an offspring that shares the nature of both parents. Furthermore, a mixture can only be made within the same *genus* <term>a broad category of classification</term> or *species* <term>a specific type within a category</term>. For example, an apple may be grafted <note>original: "graffed"</note> onto a crab-apple tree. A human might (though it is a shameful act) mate with an animal by a misuse of natural liberty <note>original Latin: *licentia naturali*</note>, but outside of their *genus* or *species*, nothing can mix. 

There are also many specific exceptions within a single *genus*. I know of many trees that the skill of man cannot graft onto one another to make them grow, yet they will grow well if grafted elsewhere. Similarly, a dog and a mouse cannot breed because they are so physically mismatched <note>original: "disproportionable"</note>. But this is an aside.

<sig>L 4</sig>

<summary>The author explains that divine law fixed all matter into specific "kinds" (genera and species), meaning that alchemical or biological mixing can only occur between related substances. This establishes the rule that "like produces like" in both nature and alchemy.</summary>

To return to our purpose, we say that just as all things are assigned their specific nature <note>original: "specificated"</note> by the will and power of God, so too—with the destruction of that *species*—the **Form** <term>the internal essence or soul of a substance that gives it its specific properties</term> (as it pertains to that individual) perishes. Since no entire *species* can cease to exist altogether, things may return to their first stable material principle—which is **Water**—through both Nature and Art. 

If Nature found this Water in a suitable place and impregnated it with a **Metallic Seed**, it might produce a **Metallic Sperm** or viscous substance. This could then become a metal through **decoction** <term>the process of refining or "ripening" a substance through steady heat</term> and provide a useful subject for our alchemical work.

### Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

->M<-

This first sign of blackness proclaims you a Master, after which you cannot easily fail, unless you choose to. This is the wonder of the Art: to make Gold volatile <note>original: "volatile"; able to be evaporated or changed into a gas, rather than remaining solid.</note>, which was previously so fixed. Be patient then, and boil continually until your Gold begins to dissolve and rises upon the water like a cream. 

Then continue your <term>decoction</term> <note>original: "decoction"; the process of heating or boiling a substance in a liquid to extract its essence.</note> until the color begins to change into an imperfect <term>Citrine</term> <note>original: "Citrine"; a lemon-yellow or orange-yellow color associated with intermediate stages in alchemy.</note> with moisture, and sends up yellowish vapors. This yellow will soon be mixed with a bluish-black; yet continue the decoction until clouds and a dark mist begin to rise. 

Then continue your boiling until "breath" fails—that is, when the clouds and fumes no longer rise. Then the Compound will boil at the bottom without fumes and will show dark, obscure, reddish, yellowish, bluish, gray, and blackish colors. 

Continue your decoction until the Body and the whole Compound begin to rot into atoms; on the 50th day, a sign of this will appear as a harbinger or forerunner, showing a pitchy blackness. Then know that all is thoroughly mingled together, and the process will never cease until the <term>damned Earth</term> <note>original: "damned Earth" (Latin: *Terra Damnata*); the useless residue or dregs left over after a substance has been distilled or purified.</note> comes, the

### Sir George Ripley's First Gate

...a tree afterward, beautiful and glorious with sprigs and branches, and changeable colors.

And indeed, the philosophers have taken the liberty to call it whatever they please <note>original: "list"</note>. They call it their "Toad," which crawls on the ground and feeds upon the slime of the Earth. This is because before it becomes completely black, it may resemble the colors and appearance of a toad—puffing, swelling, and rugged with bunches, blisters, and knobs.

Others call it a Spirit killed with its own deadly poison—that is, *Mercury* dissolving Gold. In that dissolved body (which then seems like a spirit), there is a hidden ferment <note>original: "ferment"; a substance that causes a chemical change or "leavening" in the alchemical mixture.</note> that can recongeal <note>original: "recongeal"; to solidify or thicken again.</note> the substance. It is this fermenting power that coagulates or thickens the "water," so that to the wonder of the observers <note>original: "beholders"</note>, what was previously getting thinner and thinner begins to thicken after forty days. Eventually, it becomes a dust or powder like impalpable atoms <note>original: "impalpable atoms"; particles so fine they cannot be felt or seen individually.</note>.

But I shall not dwell on these names <note>original: "denominations"</note>, as so many have been given to it by the Envious <note>original: "the Envious"; a term used by alchemists to describe authors who deliberately obscured their writings to keep secrets from the uninitiated.</note>.
