# The Vision of Sir George Ripley

<page-num>19</page-num>

-> *For a term of eighty-four days, he stood rotting.* <-

This calcination <term>the process of heating a substance until it is reduced to a powder or ash</term> begins with these variations in color around the forty-second day, or the fiftieth at the latest, under a proper regimen <term>a systematic method of managing the heat and conditions of the experiment</term>. After this comes the corruption of putrefaction <note>original: "putrefying Corruption"; the stage of decomposition</note>, which looks like the scum on boiling, bloody broth or melted pitch. However, a partial blackness—that is, a *superficial* blackness—begins about the fortieth day after the matter has been stirred up (provided there is correct progress and management of the fire), or at the latest by the fiftieth day. 

But this "drowning of him in his own poison" and "stewing him in his own broth" represents the total blackness and the absolute Cimmerian darkness <term>a reference to the mythical Cimmerians who lived in perpetual mist and gloom; here it signifies the deepest point of the *nigredo* stage</term> of complete rottenness. According to the author, this lasts for a space of eighty-four days. Alchemical authors do not all agree on this specific timeframe; however, they do all agree that one must continue the process for as long as it takes to reach completion <note>original: "Complement"</note>. One author writes, *"This blackest black lasts a long time and is not destroyed in less than five months."* Another writes,

While alchemical authorities differ on the exact duration, they agree the process must continue until the substance is completely decomposed.</summary>

...writes that the King, when he enters into his bath, pulls off his robe and gives it to Saturn <note>In alchemy, Saturn represents the planet associated with lead and the color black; here it symbolizes the start of the decomposition stage</note>, from whom he receives a black shirt which he keeps for forty-two days. Indeed, it takes forty-two days before he puts on this black shirt instead of his golden robe. This means his "solar" qualities <note>the characteristics of gold, associated with the sun</note> are destroyed. Instead of being fixed, yellow-orange <note>original: "Citrine"</note>, earthy <note>original: "Terrene"</note>, and solid, he becomes a volatile <note>original: "Fugitive"</note>, black, spiritual, watery, and phlegmatic <note>one of the four medieval humors, associated with cold and moisture</note> substance. 

But putrefaction <note>original: "Putridness"</note> does not truly begin until the original forms are shed; for as long as the body can be returned to its previous nature, it has not yet been properly ground down and soaked <note>original: "imbibed"</note>. Therefore, grind and soak the material until you see the bodies lose their physical form and become nothing but vapor <note>original: "Fume"</note> and wind. Then, after circulating for a season, you shall see them settle and rot.

Saturn then will hold the Earth—which is western <note>original: "Occidental"</note>, retentive, and autumnal—in the West. Then the process proceeds to the North, where *Mercury* holds the water; there the matter is watery and phlegmatic, and it is winter and the North...

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the alchemical timeline, specifically the transition from the black stage (Saturn) to the multicolored stage (Jupiter) and finally the white stage (Luna/the Moon). It completes the word "expulsive" from the previous page's catchword.</meta>

<page-num>21</page-num>

## the Vision of Sir G. Ripley.

...expulsive. However, those who divide the operation into **Saturn’s Rule**, followed by **Jupiter**, attribute the entire stage of putrefaction <note>original: "Putridness"; the process of decomposition or rotting of the matter</note> to Saturn, and the time of shifting colors to Jupiter. After Jupiter, who rules for only twenty or twenty-two days, comes **Luna** <note>the Moon, representing the silver or white stage of the process</note>, the third personified stage, bright and fair. She reigns for twenty full days, and sometimes two additional days. 

In this calculation, it is best to count from the fortieth or fiftieth day from the first beginning of the **Stone** <note>the Philosopher’s Stone</note> until the fourteenth or sixteenth day of Jupiter's reign. During this period, while washing the *Laton* <note>original: "Laton"; an alchemical term for the base metal or "impure" body that must be cleansed of its blackness to reach the white stage</note>, some blackness still remains, although it is mixed with a variety of bright colors. This equals the total number of days allowed by the author for putrefaction: namely, eighty-four days. 

One may account for total blackness, as **Augurellus** <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurelli (c. 1441–1524), an Italian humanist and alchemist</note> does, after forty-four days and nights (four times eleven). Or, according to another philosopher who says, "*In the first fifty days there appears the **True Crow** <note>a symbol for the black stage, or nigredo</note>, and seventy days after that, the **White Dove** <note>a symbol for the white stage, or albedo</note>.*"

<page-num>23</page-num>

# The Vision of Sir George Ripley

...spiritual nature acts upon the physical body <note>original: "Corporal"</note>, in forty-six or fifty days expect the beginning of entire blackness <note>the *nigredo* stage, representing the "death" or decomposition of the matter</note>. After fifty-six or sixty days more, expect the Peacock's Tail <note>original: "Peacocks Tayl"; an iridescent stage where the matter displays many colors like a rainbow</note> and the colors of the rainbow. 

After twenty-two or twenty-four days more, expect the perfect Moon <note>original: "Luna perfect"; the *albedo* or white stage, signifying the creation of the "White Stone" or silver</note>, the whitest white, which will grow more and more glorious for the space of twenty days, or twenty-two at the most. 

After this, in a slightly increased fire, expect the Rule of Venus <note>the Roman goddess associated with copper and certain green or multicolored hues in alchemy</note> for the space of forty or forty-two days; and after that, the Rule of Mars <note>associated with iron and the transition toward redness</note> for forty-two days more. After him, expect the Rule of the Yellow Sun <note>original: "Sol flavus"; the stage where the matter turns gold-colored</note> for forty or forty-two days. 

And then, in a moment, comes the Tyrian color <note>a deep, royal purple-red</note>, the sparkling red, the fiery vermilion, and the Red Poppy of the Rock <note>a poetic term for the final "Red Stone" or *rubedo* stage, the highest goal of alchemy</note>.

<page-num>24</page-num>
<header>An Exposition upon</header>

> *Then from the venom treated in this way, I made a medicine,*
> *Which kills venom, and saves those who happen to take venom.*

In this way, solely through boiling <term>original: Decoction; the process of heating a substance in liquid to extract its medicinal or alchemical essence</term>, these natures are changed and altered so wonderfully into this blessed elixir <term>original: Tincture</term>. This elixir drives out all poison; though it was a deadly poison itself before its preparation, afterward it is the balsam of nature. It expels all diseases and cuts them off—as if with a single hook—all those that afflict the frail human body, which is a wonder.

->*Glory be to Him, the Giver of such secret paths;*<-
->*Dominion and honor both, with worship and with praise. Amen.*<-

Now, God alone is the distributor of these glorious mysteries. I have been a true witness of nature for you, and I know that what I write is true.
